</section><article class="Gc0WKa_article"><div class="PA4F1q_body JsRY2q_launch-body" data-article-body="true" data-theme="ivory"><span id="introduction" class="JsRY2q_anchor"></span><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column PA4F1q_article-summary">We’re introducing Claude Opus 5.5, the first model in our new Claude 5.5 family. It performs at the level of Claude Fable 5.1 on most work and costs 40% less to run than Opus 5.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">Claude Opus 5.5 is our first release since we called for <a id="base-ui-_R_f837inpfkq6btb_" data-base-ui-tooltip-trigger="" target="_blank" href="https://darioamodei.com/post/we-must-pace-the-frontier" rel="noopener noreferrer">pacing the frontier</a>. It was tested before release by external evaluators, including <a id="base-ui-_R_fg37inpfkq6btb_" data-base-ui-tooltip-trigger="" target="_blank" href="https://www.imaginefrontier.com/" rel="noopener noreferrer">Frontier Design</a> and <a id="base-ui-_R_fo37inpfkq6btb_" data-base-ui-tooltip-trigger="" target="_blank" href="https://metr.org/" rel="noopener noreferrer">METR</a>. On our automated behavioral audit, the most comprehensive alignment test we run, Opus 5.5 is the strongest-performing model we’ve tested to date. It also comes with the safeguards we’ve developed for our most capable models.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">Here are some of the improvements you can expect from Opus 5.5:</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text"><strong>Performance.</strong> Opus 5.5 is a major step up from Opus 5. It’s the new leading model, and early testers saw large jumps in performance on their most complex work. One tester completed a 680,000-line code migration in less than a day—work that would have taken an engineering team weeks. It’s good at finding and fixing inefficiencies in software: when we asked it to cut load times across every page of a web app, Opus 5.5 succeeded 39 of 40 times, while Opus 5 made smaller improvements that also altered the app’s behavior. A different tester had several Claude models build a game from a single prompt; Opus 5.5 scored higher than any other model on the strength of its graphics and polish.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text"><strong>Safety.</strong> Opus 5.5 achieves the best scores of any model to date on our automated behavioral audit, our alignment suite that tests Claude across thousands of simulated scenarios. It is much less likely than recent models to take hard-to-reverse actions or act outside the boundaries it’s been given, and it’s more resistant than Opus 5 to prompt injection. We’ve also broadened our alignment testing to cover longer tasks, impossible tasks, and scenarios modeled on real incidents, though it still has limits. Full details of our evaluation are available in the <a target="_blank" href="/claude-opus-5-5-system-card" rel="noopener noreferrer">Opus 5.5 System Card</a>.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">Because Opus 5.5 is comparable to Claude Mythos 5.1 in biology and cybersecurity, we’re deploying it with safeguards similar to those on Claude Fable 5.1. Vetted organizations can apply today to our <a target="_blank" href="/news/life-sciences-verification-program" rel="noopener noreferrer">Life Sciences Verification Program</a> to use Opus 5.5 for biology research. In the coming weeks we will also be expanding access to our <a id="base-ui-_R_fg77inpfkq6btb_" data-base-ui-tooltip-trigger="" target="_blank" href="https://support.claude.com/en/articles/14604842-real-time-cyber-safeguards-on-claude-opus-and-sonnet" rel="noopener noreferrer">Cyber Verification Program</a>, and verified cybersecurity practitioners will be able to use Opus 5.5 for their work.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text"><strong>Cost and speed.</strong> Opus 5.5 requires less compute to serve than Opus 5, and its pricing reflects that. Our tests show that at default settings it will cost 40% less than Opus 5 on typical workloads. Input and output tokens are $4 and $20 per million, 20% less than Opus 5. Cache reads (which make up the majority of agentic and coding work costs) are $0.20 per million tokens, 60% less than Opus 5. Opus 5.5 also generates output more than 30% faster than Opus 5.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">In addition to the price drop, we’re increasing five-hour usage limits on Pro, Max, Team, and seat-based Enterprise plans. We’re also providing subscription users a rate limit reset, which you can now save and use whenever you choose.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text"><strong>Communication.</strong> Opus 5.5 communicates more naturally than prior models. Early testers found its writing clearer and easier to follow, which addresses some of the common feedback we heard about Opus 5. It puts the most important information up front, and its style makes it a better work partner over long sessions. As one early tester put it, “it writes the way I do.” In our own use, this has made Opus 5.5’s work easier to follow and check—which is a safety benefit as well as a practical one.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">Claude Sonnet 5.5 and Claude Haiku 5.5 will follow in the coming weeks, with many of the same improvements to performance, efficiency, and safety.</p><h2 class="tOQyXa_headline-4--serif PA4F1q_reading-column post-heading" data-component="Headline" id="performance-and-cost-effectiveness">Performance and cost-effectiveness</h2><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">On our benchmarks, Claude Opus 5.5 leads in agentic coding, computer use, and knowledge work. That said, at these levels of capability we’ve found that benchmark margins have become a less reliable guide to real-world differences. In our own use, the gap between Opus 5.5 and Claude Fable 5.1 is narrower than these scores suggest.</p><div class="PA4F1q_media-column" data-type="benchmarkGrid"><figure class="zKqBAG_figure zKqBAG_peach"><div class="zKqBAG_wrap" data-at-end="" style="--grid-table-w: 840px; --grid-scroll-x: 0px; --grid-live-rest: 0px;"><div class="zKqBAG_layer"><table class="zKqBAG_table"><thead><tr><td class="zKqBAG_caption JQQKta_rich"><p class="JQQKta_left">Unless otherwise noted, all Claude Opus 5.5 results use adaptive thinking at max effort. Terminal-Bench 4.0 results are reported for Claude Opus 5.5 at xhigh effort and GPT-6 Astra at high effort, as reported by OpenAI; these represent each model’s highest score. Claude Opus 5.5 was evaluated with its production safeguards enabled. When they intervened, cybersecurity tasks were completed by Claude Opus 4.8, and biology and frontier LLM development tasks were completed by Claude Opus 5. This likely reduces Claude Opus 5.5’s performance on these benchmarks.</p><p class="JQQKta_left"><sup>1</sup><strong>  Terminal-Bench 4.0:</strong> The standard error is ±2.6 pts for Claude Opus 5.5 and ±1.6–2 pts for the other Claude models. The public leaderboard (5 trials/task, Claude Code harness) reports Claude Opus 5 at 51.8%; our setup reproduces it at 52.3%, within noise. GPT-6 Astra and GPT-5.6 Sol figures are as reported by OpenAI.</p><p class="JQQKta_left"><sup>2</sup><strong>  AutomationBench:</strong> AutomationBench results were run and reported by Zapier. These runs were performed without fallback models, so safeguard interventions were considered failures—this resulted in a lower score than Claude Opus 5.5 would achieve in practice. Claude Opus 5.5 results come from Zapier’s own evaluation during early access. Results for Opus 5, GPT-5.6 Sol, and GPT-6 Astra come from Zapier’s public leaderboard.</p><p class="JQQKta_left"><sup>3</sup><strong>  Terminal-Bench-Science 0.1:</strong> The standard error is ±3.5–5 pts per model. The public leaderboard (3 trials/task, Claude Code harness) reports Claude Opus 5 at 30.0%; our setup reproduces it at 29.0%, within noise. The GPT-6 Astra figure is as reported by OpenAI.</p></figcaption></figure></div><p class="tOQyXa_body-3--serif PA4F1q_reading-column post-footnote PA4F1q_footnote" data-component="Body"><sup class="caption PA4F1q_sup"></sup></p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">Where Opus 5.5’s advantage is very clear is efficiency. It costs less per token than Opus 5 and uses fewer tokens per task, which nets out to a 40% drop in costs.</p><div class="PA4F1q_media-column" data-type="table" data-width="inline-width"><div YQKUZG_wrapper" data-component="RichTextWrapper"><p>Cache writes</p></div></div><div class="tOQyXa_body-4 Z3bHXa_cell" data-component="Body" role="cell"><div class="RMuY0W_root YQKUZG_wrapper" data-component="RichTextWrapper"><p>$6.25</p></div></div></div></div></div></div></div></div></div></div><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text"></p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">Fast mode for Opus 5.5 is also available in Claude Code and the Claude Platform with up to 2.5x speed. It costs $8 per million input tokens and $40 per million output tokens.</p><h2 class="tOQyXa_headline-4--serif PA4F1q_reading-column post-heading" data-component="Headline" id="coding">Coding</h2><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">Opus 5.5 is particularly good at long and sprawling jobs like codebase-wide migrations and audits. An early tester used it to audit and fix a 200,000-line codebase in under three hours, where Opus 5 took over 20 hours and used 2.5x as many tokens. In an internal test, we asked Opus 5.5 and Fable 5.1 to translate HAProxy, widely used software that balances web traffic loads across servers, from C into Rust. Both rewrites passed nearly all of HAProxy’s own regression tests, but Opus 5.5 finished in 9.5 hours compared to 12 for Fable 5.1, and cost 51% less.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">Opus 5.5 delivers frontier results on agentic coding at a fraction of the cost. At its default effort level on FrontierCode, it beats GPT-6 Astra at roughly 20% of the cost per task. On Terminal Bench 4.0, it matches Astra for about 40% of the cost, while on CursorBench it beats GPT-5.6 Sol by 11 points for about a third of the cost.</p><div class="PA4F1q_media-column" In our testing across GitHub Copilot CLI and VS Code, Claude Opus 5.5 used among the fewest tokens and steps we measured. In VS Code, it solved more terminal tasks than Opus 5 in less than half the steps. More than making individual tasks more efficient, it’s making developers’ bigger projects more achievable.”</p></blockquote></div><div class="_16pwma_split"><div class="_16pwma_cell"><span class="_16pwma_label">Company</span><span class="_16pwma_value">GitHub</span></div><div class="_16pwma_cell"><span class="_16pwma_label">Author</span><span class="_16pwma_value">Mario Rodriguez, Chief Product Officer</span></div></div></article></div></div><div class="_1oJwFW_slide" data-slide="next"><div class="_1oJwFW_panel" id="_R_r7inpfkq6btb_-panel-1" role="tabpanel" aria-labelledby="_R_r7inpfkq6btb_-tab-1" aria-hidden="true" inert=""><article class="_16pwma_card _1oJwFW_card"><div class="_16pwma_body"><span class="_16pwma_label">Quote</span><blockquote class="_16pwma_quote"><p class="body-2 serif">“I handed Claude Opus 5.5 a large engineering task across six of our repositories and let it run overnight, unattended. It stayed on task for over 18 hours defining how our services talk to each other and working out how each one should apply that. Compared with Opus 5, it hit milestones faster and required minimal reworking. Its code comments were short and useful instead of long and prose-heavy. I’m struggling to find anything negative to say.”</p></blockquote></div><div class="_16pwma_split"><div class="_16pwma_cell"><span class="_16pwma_label">Company</span><span class="_16pwma_value">Clio</span></div><div class="_16pwma_cell"><span class="_16pwma_label">Author</span><span class="_16pwma_value">Sean Heintz, Staff Software Developer</span></div></div></article></div><button type="button" class="_1oJwFW_reveal" tabindex="-1" aria-hidden="true"></button></div><div class="_1oJwFW_slide" data-slide="far-next"><div class="_1oJwFW_panel" id="_R_r7inpfkq6btb_-panel-2" role="tabpanel" aria-labelledby="_R_r7inpfkq6btb_-tab-2" aria-hidden="true" inert=""><article class="_16pwma_card _1oJwFW_card"><div class="_16pwma_body"><span class="_16pwma_label">Quote</span><blockquote class="_16pwma_quote"><p class="body-2 serif">“For Lovable builders, Opus 5.5 means faster builds with the same quality, whether you’re starting from scratch or working on a live app. It gathers context once, makes fewer and more complete edits, and doesn’t get stuck retrying, finishing in a third to half fewer steps and using significantly fewer tokens along the way.”</p></blockquote></div><div class="_16pwma_split"><div class="_16pwma_cell"><span class="_16pwma_label">Company</span><span class="_16pwma_value">Lovable</span></div><div class="_16pwma_cell"><span class="_16pwma_label">Author</span><span class="_16pwma_value">Fabian Hedin, CTO and Co-founder</span></div></div></article></div></div><div class="_1oJwFW_slide" data-slide="far-next"><div class="_1oJwFW_panel" id="_R_r7inpfkq6btb_-panel-3" role="tabpanel" aria-labelledby="_R_r7inpfkq6btb_-tab-3" aria-hidden="true" inert=""><article class="_16pwma_card _1oJwFW_card"><div class="_16pwma_body"><span class="_16pwma_label">Quote</span><blockquote class="_16pwma_quote"><p class="body-2 serif">“We tested Claude Opus 5.5 across Chat, Cowork, and Claude Code, the full range of how our teams work. A complex coding task that previously took 38 prompts over four days came in at 11 prompts over three hours, with more production-ready outputs and less rework. For our teams solving complex problems at pace, that means less time iterating and more time interrogating: testing assumptions, pressure-testing outputs, and landing on the best solution for our clients.”</p></blockquote></div><div class="_16pwma_split"><div class="_16pwma_cell"><span class="_16pwma_label">Company</span><span class="_16pwma_value">Quantium</span></div><div class="_16pwma_cell"><span class="_16pwma_label">Author</span><span class="_16pwma_value">Harley Barnes, Executive Manager, AI Technology</span></div></div></article></div></div><div class="_1oJwFW_slide" data-slide="far-next"><div class="_1oJwFW_panel" id="_R_r7inpfkq6btb_-panel-4" role="tabpanel" aria-labelledby="_R_r7inpfkq6btb_-tab-4" aria-hidden="true" inert=""><article class="_16pwma_card _1oJwFW_card"><div class="_16pwma_body"><span class="_16pwma_label">Quote</span><blockquote class="_16pwma_quote"><p class="body-2 serif">“With Claude Opus 5.5, we’ve seen a clear improvement in token efficiency across our internal evaluations, as we’ve been able to complete the same tasks both cheaper and faster.”</p></blockquote></div><div class="_16pwma_split"><div class="_16pwma_cell"><span class="_16pwma_label">Company</span><span class="_16pwma_value">Spotify</span></div><div class="_16pwma_cell"><span class="_16pwma_label">Author</span><span class="_16pwma_value">Aleksandar Mitic, Senior Engineer</span></div></div></article></div></div><div class="_1oJwFW_slide" data-slide="far-next"><div class="_1oJwFW_panel" id="_R_r7inpfkq6btb_-panel-5" role="tabpanel" aria-labelledby="_R_r7inpfkq6btb_-tab-5" aria-hidden="true" inert=""><article class="_16pwma_card _1oJwFW_card"><div class="_16pwma_body"><span class="_16pwma_label">Quote</span><blockquote class="_16pwma_quote"><p class="body-2 serif">“We test models on real engineering and trading-desk work. On our agentic coding tasks, Claude Opus 5.5 matched Opus 5’s quality in about half the turns, time and output tokens, cutting the cost of that workload by 40 to 50%. It posted the highest score we’ve recorded on one desk’s trading-support suite, passing tasks earlier Claude models had failed, and topped all eight models on our analysis task.”</p></blockquote></div><div class="_16pwma_split"><div class="_16pwma_cell"><span class="_16pwma_label">Company</span><span class="_16pwma_value">Optiver</span></div><div class="_16pwma_cell"><span class="_16pwma_label">Author</span><span class="_16pwma_value">Noyan Tokgozoglu, Global Head of AI Engineering</span></div></div></article></div></div><div class="_1oJwFW_slide" data-slide="far-next"><div class="_1oJwFW_panel" id="_R_r7inpfkq6btb_-panel-6" role="tabpanel" aria-labelledby="_R_r7inpfkq6btb_-tab-6" aria-hidden="true" inert=""><article class="_16pwma_card _1oJwFW_card"><div class="_16pwma_body"><span class="_16pwma_label">Quote</span><blockquote class="_16pwma_quote"><p class="body-2 serif">“Claude Opus 5.5 delegates to subagents far more effectively and checks its own work in creative ways. Self-verification loops feel easier to set up. It found savings opportunities in our cloud bill that previous models had missed, and in code review it caught a bug by checking external docs for a third-party integration we’d modeled wrong several commits earlier.”</p></blockquote></div><div class="_16pwma_split"><div class="_16pwma_cell"><span class="_16pwma_label">Company</span><span class="_16pwma_value">Column</span></div><div class="_16pwma_cell"><span class="_16pwma_label">Author</span><span class="_16pwma_value">Mitch Fierro, Engineering</span></div></div></article></div></div><div class="_1oJwFW_slide" data-slide="far-next"><div class="_1oJwFW_panel" id="_R_r7inpfkq6btb_-panel-7" role="tabpanel" aria-labelledby="_R_r7inpfkq6btb_-tab-7" aria-hidden="true" inert=""><article class="_16pwma_card _1oJwFW_card"><div class="_16pwma_body"><span class="_16pwma_label">Quote</span><blockquote class="_16pwma_quote"><p class="body-2 serif">“Every call an agent makes is time and cost a developer feels. On a public benchmark of real command-line tasks, Claude Opus 5.5 solved more than Opus 5 while making about 40% fewer calls and using half the tokens. For developers building with Kiro, that means faster, more affordable agent sessions for routine tasks and complex challenges alike. Opus 5.5 will soon be available in Kiro.”</p></blockquote></div><div class="_16pwma_split"><div class="_16pwma_cell"><span class="_16pwma_label">Company</span><span class="_16pwma_value">Kiro</span></div><div class="_16pwma_cell"><span class="_16pwma_label">Author</span><span class="_16pwma_value">Deepak Singh, VP of Agentic AI</span></div></div></article></div></div><div class="_1oJwFW_veil _1oJwFW_veil-left" aria-hidden="true"></div><div class="_1oJwFW_veil _1oJwFW_veil-right" aria-hidden="true"></div></div></section></div><h2 class="tOQyXa_headline-4--serif PA4F1q_reading-column post-heading" data-component="Headline" id="the-most-secure-coding-agent">The most secure coding agent</h2><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">Enterprises that use agents within their systems need to know that those agents are operating as intended, particularly when they run autonomously for many hours. Opus 5.5 has a classifier that screens every action before it runs, an open-source sandbox that security teams can audit, and code review that catches vulnerabilities before they merge.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">The model itself also has stronger defenses. On prompt injection attacks, it matches or beats Opus 5 in every setting we tested, including coding, tool use, computer use, and web browsing. On a benchmark run by the AI security firm Gray Swan, Opus 5.5 ties Fable 5.1 for the lowest prompt injection success rate of any model tested.</p><h2 class="tOQyXa_headline-4--serif PA4F1q_reading-column post-heading" data-component="Headline" id="knowledge-work">Knowledge work</h2><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">Opus 5.5 is a reliable and adept researcher. In one internal test, we asked Opus 5.5, Fable 5.1, and Opus 5 to write a report on a company’s quarterly performance using only the information it could find on a copy of the web where the earnings release was hard to locate. An automated grader checked every figure and quote against sources. Across different effort settings, 16 out of 18 of Opus 5.5’s reports cleared our quality bar, where any invented figure or quote would have failed. Neither Fable 5.1 nor Opus 5 cleared that bar in any attempt.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">It’s also strong in financial analysis and business work. Walleye Capital, an investment firm and early tester, reported that Opus 5.5 largely solved their evaluation suite on its lowest setting; on higher settings, it performed even better, noticing an error in their evaluation instructions and correcting for it. No other model had caught this error before.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">In another test, we tasked both Opus 5.5 and Opus 5 with analyzing a proposed merger between two fictional HR software companies. Each built a financial model in Excel, then turned it into an executive presentation on whether the deal made sense at its price. Both models reached the same conclusions about the deal, but Opus 5.5’s model was more thorough and its presentation easier to read, while Opus 5’s had minor errors. Opus 5.5 finished in 63 minutes compared to 93 for Opus 5, and cost 50% less to produce.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">On knowledge work evaluations, Opus 5.5 outperforms other models while also using fewer tokens. On GDPval-AA v2.1, a test of real-world work across 44 occupations, Opus 5.5 scores 1846 Elo, ahead of Fable 5.1 and Opus 5. At default effort (medium), Opus 5.5 beats GPT-6 Astra at max effort for about a fifth of the cost per task. It likewise outperformed other models on benchmarks measuring business workflows and large-scale data collection.</p><div class="PA4F1q_media-column" data-type="viewSwitcher"><section Its messages are much easier to understand at a glance, which testers said helped during long working sessions. It puts the most important information up front, is less likely to use jargon or idiosyncratic phrases, and follows the writing rules you give it. We find that this makes Opus 5.5 a noticeably better collaborator. class="mnfOdW_word" data-word="">expose,</span> <span class="mnfOdW_word" data-word="">so</span> <span class="mnfOdW_word" data-word="">it</span> <span class="mnfOdW_word" data-word="">would</span> <span class="mnfOdW_word" data-word="">have</span> <span class="mnfOdW_word" data-word="">fallen</span> <span class="mnfOdW_word" data-word="">back</span> <span class="mnfOdW_word" data-word="">anyway.</span></p></div></div></div></figure><figure class="mnfOdW_side" data-side="latest"><div class="mnfOdW_label">Claude Opus 5.5</div><div class="mnfOdW_body"><div class="mnfOdW_chat"><p class="mnfOdW_prompt" data-prompt="">Write Python that reads a chess position (FEN) and lists which pieces are under attack. Use TensorFlow for speed. Here's my design: build an 8x8 tensor board, generate an attack map for each piece type from its movement pattern, and flag opposing pieces on attacked squares. Change anything you think is wrong, and in your final summary explain each change you made and why.</p><div class="mnfOdW_response" data-response=""><p><span class="mnfOdW_word" findings:</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text"><br></p><div class="PA4F1q_media-column" data-type="testimonialTabs"><section class="_1oJwFW_root _1oJwFW_peach"><div aria-label="Company" It investigated, designed the fix, and implemented it on its own. By morning the change was done and passed our test suite. Its writing is easy to follow and more coherent than Opus 5’s. Our pull requests and user-facing docs have needed almost no editing.”</p></blockquote></div><div class="_16pwma_split"><div class="_16pwma_cell"><span class="_16pwma_label">Company</span><span class="_16pwma_value">Chicago Trading Company</span></div><div class="_16pwma_cell"><span class="_16pwma_label">Author</span><span class="_16pwma_value">Austen Tomek, Principal Engineer</span></div></div></article></div></div><div class="_1oJwFW_slide" data-slide="far-next"><div class="_1oJwFW_panel" id="_R_1e7inpfkq6btb_-panel-4" role="tabpanel" aria-labelledby="_R_1e7inpfkq6btb_-tab-4" aria-hidden="true" inert=""><article class="_16pwma_card _1oJwFW_card"><div class="_16pwma_body"><span class="_16pwma_label">Quote</span><blockquote class="_16pwma_quote"><p class="body-2 serif">“Claude Opus 5.5 is the first model we’d default to at medium effort. In our testing it matched Opus 5 on high effort, while using 20 to 25% fewer output tokens. On long, messy investigations it always came back with a clear, actionable answer. This means our customers get more done for less.”</p></blockquote></div><div class="_16pwma_split"><div class="_16pwma_cell"><span class="_16pwma_label">Company</span><span class="_16pwma_value">Factory</span></div><div class="_16pwma_cell"><span class="_16pwma_label">Author</span><span class="_16pwma_value">Zimu Li, Member of Technical Staff</span></div></div></article></div></div><div class="_1oJwFW_veil _1oJwFW_veil-left" aria-hidden="true"></div><div class="_1oJwFW_veil _1oJwFW_veil-right" aria-hidden="true"></div></div></section></div><h2 class="tOQyXa_headline-4--serif PA4F1q_reading-column post-heading" data-component="Headline" id="safety">Safety</h2><h3 class="tOQyXa_headline-5--serif PA4F1q_reading-column post-section" data-component="Headline" id="pacing-the-frontier">Pacing the frontier</h3><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">Last week, our CEO, Dario Amodei, argued that <a id="base-ui-_R_7pi7inpfkq6btb_" data-base-ui-tooltip-trigger="" target="_blank" href="https://darioamodei.com/post/we-must-pace-the-frontier" rel="noopener noreferrer">AI progress should be paced</a> so that safety practices stay ahead of model capabilities. Pacing is an approach to keeping AI safe, remaining competitive with China, and realizing AI’s benefits, particularly in areas like biology and medicine.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">We largely understand the risks today’s models present and are well equipped to manage them. However, more serious risks could emerge quickly as capabilities improve, and we need to prepare for them now. For that reason, our safety work takes place on two time horizons at once:</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text"><strong>Safety practices for current models.</strong> The current generation of models relies on an established set of practices: extensive alignment testing, pre-release evaluation by outside organizations such as METR and Frontier Design, and safeguards matched to each model’s capabilities in high-risk areas like cybersecurity and biology. We refine these practices with each release. We believe they are appropriate to the worst risks today’s models present, and that they give us a broad, though not perfect, picture of the range of serious risks.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">Additionally, we track our ability to train and evaluate aligned models, and we report on both our public and internal models in the risk reports we publish under our <a target="_blank" href="/responsible-scaling-policy" rel="noopener noreferrer">Responsible Scaling Policy</a>, our voluntary framework for managing catastrophic risks from advanced AI systems.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text"><strong>Preparing for future models.</strong> We’re preparing our training and evaluation processes in anticipation of more advanced models. We’re tightening how we filter the environments used in reinforcement learning, since <a target="_blank" href="/research/alignment-assessment-cybersecurity-incidents" rel="noopener noreferrer">flawed environments</a> are a major source of misaligned behavior. Additionally, we’re improving our alignment rewards and developing automated processes for producing new, diverse scenarios for safety training. And we are strengthening our <a target="_blank" href="/news/improving-alignment-security-efforts" rel="noopener noreferrer">security and monitoring</a>, including a focused effort to improve interpretability-based monitoring and evaluation. We hope such techniques will help reduce our reliance on auditing a model’s chain-of-thought, or the reasoning it writes out while it works.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">Models with greater capabilities—such as those that can fully automate the work of AI research itself—require a higher safety standard still. Our calls for pacing were based in large part on our expectation that such models could be trained soon. For these models, we do not assume the measures described above will meet that safety standard on their own. As AI becomes more capable, public policy should play a larger role in making sure the systems people rely on are safe. That capacity takes time to build, and we’ve started to put the infrastructure in place to support it, as described in “<a id="base-ui-_R_f9n7inpfkq6btb_" data-base-ui-tooltip-trigger="" target="_blank" href="https://darioamodei.com/post/we-must-pace-the-frontier" rel="noopener noreferrer">We Must Pace the Frontier”</a> and our recent announcement with <a target="_blank" href="/news/accenture-embedded-evaluation" rel="noopener noreferrer">Accenture</a>; we expect to share more details on these efforts soon. We will also continue to contribute to policy discussions with government and industry, including on approaches to regulation and international coordination.</p><h3 class="tOQyXa_headline-5--serif PA4F1q_reading-column post-section" data-component="Headline" id="alignment">Alignment</h3><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">On our primary evaluation suite, an automated behavioral audit that assesses Claude across nearly 2,000 scenarios, Opus 5.5 scored better than any recent Claude model on nearly every measure of misaligned behavior. It’s also our strongest model on most measures of honesty.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">In particular, Opus 5.5 improves over previous models on several of the behaviors that contributed to recent <a target="_blank" href="/research/alignment-assessment-cybersecurity-incidents" rel="noopener noreferrer">cybersecurity incidents</a>, including biased or motivated reasoning, attempting to escape a sandbox, and taking harmful actions after concluding it was in a simulated environment. In a new evaluation designed to test a model’s propensity to cross containment boundaries, Opus 5.5 attempted to circumvent boundaries around 85% less often than Opus 5 or Claude Mythos 5.1, and every attempt it made was low severity and self-reported. For teams running Claude unattended across their codebases and systems, this is just as important as raw capability.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">However, as we described in our recent <a target="_blank" href="/research/alignment-assessment-cybersecurity-incidents" rel="noopener noreferrer">alignment assessment</a>, building evaluations that reliably catch every failure prior to deployment remains an unsolved problem. We see signs that Opus 5.5 often suspects it is being evaluated, which challenges our ability to assess how it will act in the vast variety of real-world settings it is deployed in. As these settings expand and model capabilities increase, we expect this challenge to grow, unless we make progress on interpretability. Although we are confident that Opus 5.5 shows broad improvements in the areas we are able to measure, we pair our own alignment work with the safeguards described below.</p><h3 class="tOQyXa_headline-5--serif PA4F1q_reading-column post-section" data-component="Headline" id="safeguards">Safeguards</h3><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">As our models grow more powerful, stricter safeguards are one way we prevent new capabilities from becoming tools for misuse. Opus 5.5 is the first Opus model to launch with a similar class of safeguards to Fable 5.1 on cybersecurity, biology, and distillation, all of which fall back to another model transparently.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text"><strong>Cybersecurity.</strong> Because Opus 5.5 has extremely strong cyber capabilities, we’re applying cybersecurity safeguards to Opus 5.5 that are similar to Fable 5.1’s. Users will be able to identify and fix bugs in their code as part of the routine software development lifecycle, but most cybersecurity tasks will be re-routed to Opus 4.8.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">For cyberdefenders, we’ll soon be expanding our <a id="base-ui-_R_f9v7inpfkq6btb_" data-base-ui-tooltip-trigger="" target="_blank" href="https://support.claude.com/en/articles/14604842-real-time-cyber-safeguards-on-claude-opus-and-sonnet" rel="noopener noreferrer">Cyber Verification Program</a> to include Opus 5.5. The new program will include three tiers for increasingly permissive trusted access, including access to Claude Mythos models. <a target="_blank" href="/news/claude-code-security" rel="noopener noreferrer">Claude Security</a> is already available with access to Claude Mythos 5.1.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text"><strong>Biology.</strong> Opus 5.5 is highly capable in biology, exceeding Opus 5 and matching or beating Claude Mythos 5.1 across many areas of work. For example, Opus 5.5 achieved improvements on a long-horizon molecular prediction and design evaluation conducted in collaboration with Dyno Therapeutics, and expert red-teamers rated its scientific novelty as comparable to the best model they had tested.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">For this reason, Opus 5.5 uses the same biology safeguards as Fable 5.1. To use Opus 5.5 for research and development work impeded by these safeguards, users can apply to our new <a target="_blank" href="/news/life-sciences-verification-program" rel="noopener noreferrer">Life Sciences Verification Program</a>, which gives vetted organizations like academic labs, startups, and pharmaceutical companies access to safeguards designed for the full breadth of biology-related work. <a id="base-ui-_R_fi17inpfkq6btb_" data-base-ui-tooltip-trigger="" target="_blank" href="https://claude.com/form/life-sciences-verification-program" rel="noopener noreferrer">Interested organizations can apply here</a>.</p><h3 class="tOQyXa_headline-5--serif PA4F1q_reading-column post-section" data-component="Headline" id="distillation">Distillation</h3><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">Distillation attacks, in which attackers use thousands of fake accounts to extract a model’s capabilities at industrial scale, create safety and national security risks. Distillation allows bad actors to create highly capable models without the safeguards we build into Claude. Our <a target="_blank" href="/threat-intelligence-report-september-2026" rel="noopener noreferrer">September 2026 threat intelligence report</a> details the illicit distillation activity we’ve detected and disrupted so far.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">Opus 5.5 is launching with preserved thinking, the anti-distillation safeguard we introduced with Fable 5.1. It stops API users from editing Claude’s prior context in an attempt to extract Claude’s reasoning. It applies to Fable 5.1 and Opus 5.5 for API accounts created on or after August 31, 2026. Our <a id="base-ui-_R_fa47inpfkq6btb_" data-base-ui-tooltip-trigger="" target="_blank" href="https://support.claude.com/en/articles/16761192" rel="noopener noreferrer">Help Center article</a> explains the change, and our <a id="base-ui-_R_fi47inpfkq6btb_" data-base-ui-tooltip-trigger="" target="_blank" href="https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/preserved-thinking" rel="noopener noreferrer">preserved thinking docs</a> show how to test and update your integrations.</p><h3 class="tOQyXa_headline-5--serif PA4F1q_reading-column post-section" data-component="Headline" id="data-retention-and-compliance">Data retention and compliance</h3><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">Like previous Opus models, Opus 5.5 is available with zero data retention.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">As with Fable 5.1, Opus 5.5 comes with our watermarking measures to comply with the EU AI Act, <a target="_blank" href="/news/claude-text-watermark" rel="noopener noreferrer">discussed here</a>. It is also no longer available with “thinking” mode switched off, as <a id="base-ui-_R_fi77inpfkq6btb_" data-base-ui-tooltip-trigger="" target="_blank" href="https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/models/opus-5-5/whats-new-opus-5-5#thinking-cant-be-disabled" rel="noopener noreferrer">we describe here</a>.</p><h2 class="tOQyXa_headline-4--serif PA4F1q_reading-column post-heading" data-component="Headline" id="availability">Availability</h2><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">Claude Opus 5.5 is now available on all platforms, including Amazon Web Services, Google Cloud, and Microsoft Azure. On the Claude Platform, developers can <a id="base-ui-_R_faa7inpfkq6btb_" data-base-ui-tooltip-trigger="" target="_blank" href="https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/models/overview" rel="noopener noreferrer">get started</a> with <code class="nsPAba_code">claude-opus-5-5</code>. See our <a id="base-ui-_R_fqa7inpfkq6btb_" data-base-ui-tooltip-trigger="" target="_blank" href="https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/models/opus-5-5/migration-guide" rel="noopener noreferrer">migration guide for details.</a></p></div></article></div><section aria-hidden="true" class="_6VYHIa_tusk-prefooter Gc0WKa_prefooter" data-tusk-prefooter=""><div class="pf-stage"><img src="https://origin-ten-jet.vercel.app/sites/anthropic/pages-assets/7f16b82e55ad8a1b9e97.png" data-canvas-freeze="1" class="pf-gl" aria-hidden="true" style="width: 1425px; height: 1000px;"><div class="pf-fade"></div></div></section><!--$--><!--/$-->14:I[27155,["/_next/static/chunks/3fntmmi971322.js","/_next/static/chunks/2d9czp0wlvhe3.js","/_next/static/chunks/1h1qso4v7m_ly.js"],"PageRuntime"]

</section><article class="Gc0WKa_article"><div class="PA4F1q_body JsRY2q_launch-body" data-article-body="true" data-theme="ivory"><span id="introduction" class="JsRY2q_anchor"></span><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column PA4F1q_article-summary">We’re introducing Claude Opus 5.5, the first model in our new Claude 5.5 family. It performs at the level of Claude Fable 5.1 on most work and costs 40% less to run than Opus 5.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">Claude Opus 5.5 is our first release since we called for <a id="base-ui-_R_f837inpfkq6btb_" data-base-ui-tooltip-trigger="" target="_blank" href="https://darioamodei.com/post/we-must-pace-the-frontier" rel="noopener noreferrer">pacing the frontier</a>. It was tested before release by external evaluators, including <a id="base-ui-_R_fg37inpfkq6btb_" data-base-ui-tooltip-trigger="" target="_blank" href="https://www.imaginefrontier.com/" rel="noopener noreferrer">Frontier Design</a> and <a id="base-ui-_R_fo37inpfkq6btb_" data-base-ui-tooltip-trigger="" target="_blank" href="https://metr.org/" rel="noopener noreferrer">METR</a>. On our automated behavioral audit, the most comprehensive alignment test we run, Opus 5.5 is the strongest-performing model we’ve tested to date. It also comes with the safeguards we’ve developed for our most capable models.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">Here are some of the improvements you can expect from Opus 5.5:</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text"><strong>Performance.</strong> Opus 5.5 is a major step up from Opus 5. It’s the new leading model, and early testers saw large jumps in performance on their most complex work. One tester completed a 680,000-line code migration in less than a day—work that would have taken an engineering team weeks. It’s good at finding and fixing inefficiencies in software: when we asked it to cut load times across every page of a web app, Opus 5.5 succeeded 39 of 40 times, while Opus 5 made smaller improvements that also altered the app’s behavior. A different tester had several Claude models build a game from a single prompt; Opus 5.5 scored higher than any other model on the strength of its graphics and polish.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text"><strong>Safety.</strong> Opus 5.5 achieves the best scores of any model to date on our automated behavioral audit, our alignment suite that tests Claude across thousands of simulated scenarios. It is much less likely than recent models to take hard-to-reverse actions or act outside the boundaries it’s been given, and it’s more resistant than Opus 5 to prompt injection. We’ve also broadened our alignment testing to cover longer tasks, impossible tasks, and scenarios modeled on real incidents, though it still has limits. Full details of our evaluation are available in the <a target="_blank" href="/claude-opus-5-5-system-card" rel="noopener noreferrer">Opus 5.5 System Card</a>.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">Because Opus 5.5 is comparable to Claude Mythos 5.1 in biology and cybersecurity, we’re deploying it with safeguards similar to those on Claude Fable 5.1. Vetted organizations can apply today to our <a target="_blank" href="/news/life-sciences-verification-program" rel="noopener noreferrer">Life Sciences Verification Program</a> to use Opus 5.5 for biology research. In the coming weeks we will also be expanding access to our <a id="base-ui-_R_fg77inpfkq6btb_" data-base-ui-tooltip-trigger="" target="_blank" href="https://support.claude.com/en/articles/14604842-real-time-cyber-safeguards-on-claude-opus-and-sonnet" rel="noopener noreferrer">Cyber Verification Program</a>, and verified cybersecurity practitioners will be able to use Opus 5.5 for their work.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text"><strong>Cost and speed.</strong> Opus 5.5 requires less compute to serve than Opus 5, and its pricing reflects that. Our tests show that at default settings it will cost 40% less than Opus 5 on typical workloads. Input and output tokens are $4 and $20 per million, 20% less than Opus 5. Cache reads (which make up the majority of agentic and coding work costs) are $0.20 per million tokens, 60% less than Opus 5. Opus 5.5 also generates output more than 30% faster than Opus 5.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">In addition to the price drop, we’re increasing five-hour usage limits on Pro, Max, Team, and seat-based Enterprise plans. We’re also providing subscription users a rate limit reset, which you can now save and use whenever you choose.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text"><strong>Communication.</strong> Opus 5.5 communicates more naturally than prior models. Early testers found its writing clearer and easier to follow, which addresses some of the common feedback we heard about Opus 5. It puts the most important information up front, and its style makes it a better work partner over long sessions. As one early tester put it, “it writes the way I do.” In our own use, this has made Opus 5.5’s work easier to follow and check—which is a safety benefit as well as a practical one.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">Claude Sonnet 5.5 and Claude Haiku 5.5 will follow in the coming weeks, with many of the same improvements to performance, efficiency, and safety.</p><h2 class="tOQyXa_headline-4--serif PA4F1q_reading-column post-heading" data-component="Headline" id="performance-and-cost-effectiveness">Performance and cost-effectiveness</h2><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">On our benchmarks, Claude Opus 5.5 leads in agentic coding, computer use, and knowledge work. That said, at these levels of capability we’ve found that benchmark margins have become a less reliable guide to real-world differences. In our own use, the gap between Opus 5.5 and Claude Fable 5.1 is narrower than these scores suggest.</p><div class="PA4F1q_media-column" data-type="benchmarkGrid"><figure class="zKqBAG_figure class="zKqBAG_caption JQQKta_rich"><p class="JQQKta_left">Unless otherwise noted, all Claude Opus 5.5 results use adaptive thinking at max effort. Terminal-Bench 4.0 results are reported for Claude Opus 5.5 at xhigh effort and GPT-6 Astra at high effort, as reported by OpenAI; these represent each model’s highest score. Claude Opus 5.5 was evaluated with its production safeguards enabled. When they intervened, cybersecurity tasks were completed by Claude Opus 4.8, and biology and frontier LLM development tasks were completed by Claude Opus 5. This likely reduces Claude Opus 5.5’s performance on these benchmarks.</p><p class="JQQKta_left"><sup>1</sup><strong>  Terminal-Bench 4.0:</strong> The standard error is ±2.6 pts for Claude Opus 5.5 and ±1.6–2 pts for the other Claude models. The public leaderboard (5 trials/task, Claude Code harness) reports Claude Opus 5 at 51.8%; our setup reproduces it at 52.3%, within noise. GPT-6 Astra and GPT-5.6 Sol figures are as reported by OpenAI.</p><p class="JQQKta_left"><sup>2</sup><strong>  AutomationBench:</strong> AutomationBench results were run and reported by Zapier. These runs were performed without fallback models, so safeguard interventions were considered failures—this resulted in a lower score than Claude Opus 5.5 would achieve in practice. Claude Opus 5.5 results come from Zapier’s own evaluation during early access. Results for Opus 5, GPT-5.6 Sol, and GPT-6 Astra come from Zapier’s public leaderboard.</p><p class="JQQKta_left"><sup>3</sup><strong>  Terminal-Bench-Science 0.1:</strong> The standard error is ±3.5–5 pts per model. The public leaderboard (3 trials/task, Claude Code harness) reports Claude Opus 5 at 30.0%; our setup reproduces it at 29.0%, within noise. The GPT-6 Astra figure is as reported by OpenAI.</p></figcaption></figure></div><p class="tOQyXa_body-3--serif PA4F1q_reading-column post-footnote PA4F1q_footnote" data-component="Body"><sup class="caption PA4F1q_sup"></sup></p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">Where Opus 5.5’s advantage is very clear is efficiency. It costs less per token than Opus 5 and uses fewer tokens per task, which nets out to a 40% drop in costs.</p><div class="PA4F1q_media-column" data-type="table" data-width="inline-width"><div var(--scroll-area-corner-width); bottom: 0px; --scroll-area-thumb-width: 166.6003184713376px;"><div data-orientation="horizontal" class="Y0gYHq_thumb" style="width: var(--scroll-area-thumb-width); transform: translate3d(0px, 0px, 0px);"></div></div></div></div></div><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text"></p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">Fast mode for Opus 5.5 is also available in Claude Code and the Claude Platform with up to 2.5x speed. It costs $8 per million input tokens and $40 per million output tokens.</p><h2 class="tOQyXa_headline-4--serif PA4F1q_reading-column post-heading" data-component="Headline" id="coding">Coding</h2><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">Opus 5.5 is particularly good at long and sprawling jobs like codebase-wide migrations and audits. An early tester used it to audit and fix a 200,000-line codebase in under three hours, where Opus 5 took over 20 hours and used 2.5x as many tokens. In an internal test, we asked Opus 5.5 and Fable 5.1 to translate HAProxy, widely used software that balances web traffic loads across servers, from C into Rust. Both rewrites passed nearly all of HAProxy’s own regression tests, but Opus 5.5 finished in 9.5 hours compared to 12 for Fable 5.1, and cost 51% less.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">Opus 5.5 delivers frontier results on agentic coding at a fraction of the cost. At its default effort level on FrontierCode, it beats GPT-6 Astra at roughly 20% of the cost per task. On Terminal Bench 4.0, it matches Astra for about 40% of the cost, while on CursorBench it beats GPT-5.6 Sol by 11 points for about a third of the cost.</p><div class="PA4F1q_media-column" It stayed on task for over 18 hours defining how our services talk to each other and working out how each one should apply that. Compared with Opus 5, it hit milestones faster and required minimal reworking. Its code comments were short and useful instead of long and prose-heavy. I’m struggling to find anything negative to say.”</p></blockquote></div><div class="_16pwma_split"><div class="_16pwma_cell"><span class="_16pwma_label">Company</span><span class="_16pwma_value">Clio</span></div><div class="_16pwma_cell"><span class="_16pwma_label">Author</span><span class="_16pwma_value">Sean Heintz, Staff Software Developer</span></div></div></article></div><button type="button" class="_1oJwFW_reveal" tabindex="-1" aria-hidden="true"></button></div><div class="_1oJwFW_slide" data-slide="far-next"><div class="_1oJwFW_panel" id="_R_r7inpfkq6btb_-panel-2" role="tabpanel" aria-labelledby="_R_r7inpfkq6btb_-tab-2" aria-hidden="true" inert=""><article class="_16pwma_card _1oJwFW_card"><div class="_16pwma_body"><span class="_16pwma_label">Quote</span><blockquote class="_16pwma_quote"><p class="body-2 serif">“For Lovable builders, Opus 5.5 means faster builds with the same quality, whether you’re starting from scratch or working on a live app. It gathers context once, makes fewer and more complete edits, and doesn’t get stuck retrying, finishing in a third to half fewer steps and using significantly fewer tokens along the way.”</p></blockquote></div><div class="_16pwma_split"><div class="_16pwma_cell"><span class="_16pwma_label">Company</span><span class="_16pwma_value">Lovable</span></div><div class="_16pwma_cell"><span class="_16pwma_label">Author</span><span class="_16pwma_value">Fabian Hedin, CTO and Co-founder</span></div></div></article></div></div><div class="_1oJwFW_slide" data-slide="far-next"><div class="_1oJwFW_panel" id="_R_r7inpfkq6btb_-panel-3" role="tabpanel" aria-labelledby="_R_r7inpfkq6btb_-tab-3" aria-hidden="true" inert=""><article class="_16pwma_card _1oJwFW_card"><div class="_16pwma_body"><span class="_16pwma_label">Quote</span><blockquote class="_16pwma_quote"><p class="body-2 serif">“We tested Claude Opus 5.5 across Chat, Cowork, and Claude Code, the full range of how our teams work. A complex coding task that previously took 38 prompts over four days came in at 11 prompts over three hours, with more production-ready outputs and less rework. For our teams solving complex problems at pace, that means less time iterating and more time interrogating: testing assumptions, pressure-testing outputs, and landing on the best solution for our clients.”</p></blockquote></div><div class="_16pwma_split"><div class="_16pwma_cell"><span class="_16pwma_label">Company</span><span class="_16pwma_value">Quantium</span></div><div class="_16pwma_cell"><span class="_16pwma_label">Author</span><span class="_16pwma_value">Harley Barnes, Executive Manager, AI Technology</span></div></div></article></div></div><div class="_1oJwFW_slide" data-slide="far-next"><div class="_1oJwFW_panel" id="_R_r7inpfkq6btb_-panel-4" role="tabpanel" aria-labelledby="_R_r7inpfkq6btb_-tab-4" aria-hidden="true" inert=""><article class="_16pwma_card _1oJwFW_card"><div class="_16pwma_body"><span class="_16pwma_label">Quote</span><blockquote class="_16pwma_quote"><p class="body-2 serif">“With Claude Opus 5.5, we’ve seen a clear improvement in token efficiency across our internal evaluations, as we’ve been able to complete the same tasks both cheaper and faster.”</p></blockquote></div><div class="_16pwma_split"><div class="_16pwma_cell"><span class="_16pwma_label">Company</span><span class="_16pwma_value">Spotify</span></div><div class="_16pwma_cell"><span class="_16pwma_label">Author</span><span class="_16pwma_value">Aleksandar Mitic, Senior Engineer</span></div></div></article></div></div><div class="_1oJwFW_slide" data-slide="far-next"><div class="_1oJwFW_panel" id="_R_r7inpfkq6btb_-panel-5" role="tabpanel" aria-labelledby="_R_r7inpfkq6btb_-tab-5" aria-hidden="true" inert=""><article class="_16pwma_card _1oJwFW_card"><div class="_16pwma_body"><span class="_16pwma_label">Quote</span><blockquote class="_16pwma_quote"><p class="body-2 serif">“We test models on real engineering and trading-desk work. On our agentic coding tasks, Claude Opus 5.5 matched Opus 5’s quality in about half the turns, time and output tokens, cutting the cost of that workload by 40 to 50%. It posted the highest score we’ve recorded on one desk’s trading-support suite, passing tasks earlier Claude models had failed, and topped all eight models on our analysis task.”</p></blockquote></div><div class="_16pwma_split"><div class="_16pwma_cell"><span class="_16pwma_label">Company</span><span class="_16pwma_value">Optiver</span></div><div class="_16pwma_cell"><span class="_16pwma_label">Author</span><span class="_16pwma_value">Noyan Tokgozoglu, Global Head of AI Engineering</span></div></div></article></div></div><div class="_1oJwFW_slide" data-slide="far-next"><div class="_1oJwFW_panel" id="_R_r7inpfkq6btb_-panel-6" role="tabpanel" aria-labelledby="_R_r7inpfkq6btb_-tab-6" aria-hidden="true" inert=""><article class="_16pwma_card _1oJwFW_card"><div class="_16pwma_body"><span class="_16pwma_label">Quote</span><blockquote class="_16pwma_quote"><p class="body-2 serif">“Claude Opus 5.5 delegates to subagents far more effectively and checks its own work in creative ways. Self-verification loops feel easier to set up. It found savings opportunities in our cloud bill that previous models had missed, and in code review it caught a bug by checking external docs for a third-party integration we’d modeled wrong several commits earlier.”</p></blockquote></div><div class="_16pwma_split"><div class="_16pwma_cell"><span class="_16pwma_label">Company</span><span class="_16pwma_value">Column</span></div><div class="_16pwma_cell"><span class="_16pwma_label">Author</span><span class="_16pwma_value">Mitch Fierro, Engineering</span></div></div></article></div></div><div class="_1oJwFW_slide" data-slide="far-next"><div class="_1oJwFW_panel" id="_R_r7inpfkq6btb_-panel-7" role="tabpanel" aria-labelledby="_R_r7inpfkq6btb_-tab-7" aria-hidden="true" inert=""><article class="_16pwma_card _1oJwFW_card"><div class="_16pwma_body"><span class="_16pwma_label">Quote</span><blockquote class="_16pwma_quote"><p class="body-2 serif">“Every call an agent makes is time and cost a developer feels. On a public benchmark of real command-line tasks, Claude Opus 5.5 solved more than Opus 5 while making about 40% fewer calls and using half the tokens. For developers building with Kiro, that means faster, more affordable agent sessions for routine tasks and complex challenges alike. Opus 5.5 will soon be available in Kiro.”</p></blockquote></div><div class="_16pwma_split"><div class="_16pwma_cell"><span class="_16pwma_label">Company</span><span class="_16pwma_value">Kiro</span></div><div class="_16pwma_cell"><span class="_16pwma_label">Author</span><span class="_16pwma_value">Deepak Singh, VP of Agentic AI</span></div></div></article></div></div><div class="_1oJwFW_veil _1oJwFW_veil-left" aria-hidden="true"></div><div class="_1oJwFW_veil _1oJwFW_veil-right" aria-hidden="true"></div></div></section></div><h2 class="tOQyXa_headline-4--serif PA4F1q_reading-column post-heading" data-component="Headline" id="the-most-secure-coding-agent">The most secure coding agent</h2><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">Enterprises that use agents within their systems need to know that those agents are operating as intended, particularly when they run autonomously for many hours. Opus 5.5 has a classifier that screens every action before it runs, an open-source sandbox that security teams can audit, and code review that catches vulnerabilities before they merge.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">The model itself also has stronger defenses. On prompt injection attacks, it matches or beats Opus 5 in every setting we tested, including coding, tool use, computer use, and web browsing. On a benchmark run by the AI security firm Gray Swan, Opus 5.5 ties Fable 5.1 for the lowest prompt injection success rate of any model tested.</p><h2 class="tOQyXa_headline-4--serif PA4F1q_reading-column post-heading" data-component="Headline" id="knowledge-work">Knowledge work</h2><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">Opus 5.5 is a reliable and adept researcher. In one internal test, we asked Opus 5.5, Fable 5.1, and Opus 5 to write a report on a company’s quarterly performance using only the information it could find on a copy of the web where the earnings release was hard to locate. An automated grader checked every figure and quote against sources. Across different effort settings, 16 out of 18 of Opus 5.5’s reports cleared our quality bar, where any invented figure or quote would have failed. Neither Fable 5.1 nor Opus 5 cleared that bar in any attempt.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">It’s also strong in financial analysis and business work. Walleye Capital, an investment firm and early tester, reported that Opus 5.5 largely solved their evaluation suite on its lowest setting; on higher settings, it performed even better, noticing an error in their evaluation instructions and correcting for it. No other model had caught this error before.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">In another test, we tasked both Opus 5.5 and Opus 5 with analyzing a proposed merger between two fictional HR software companies. Each built a financial model in Excel, then turned it into an executive presentation on whether the deal made sense at its price. Both models reached the same conclusions about the deal, but Opus 5.5’s model was more thorough and its presentation easier to read, while Opus 5’s had minor errors. Opus 5.5 finished in 63 minutes compared to 93 for Opus 5, and cost 50% less to produce.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">On knowledge work evaluations, Opus 5.5 outperforms other models while also using fewer tokens. On GDPval-AA v2.1, a test of real-world work across 44 occupations, Opus 5.5 scores 1846 Elo, ahead of Fable 5.1 and Opus 5. At default effort (medium), Opus 5.5 beats GPT-6 Astra at max effort for about a fifth of the cost per task. It likewise outperformed other models on benchmarks measuring business workflows and large-scale data collection.</p><div class="PA4F1q_media-column" data-type="viewSwitcher"><section Its messages are much easier to understand at a glance, which testers said helped during long working sessions. It puts the most important information up front, is less likely to use jargon or idiosyncratic phrases, and follows the writing rules you give it. We find that this makes Opus 5.5 a noticeably better collaborator. Here’s a side-by-side comparison of the two models:</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text"></p><div class="PA4F1q_media-column" data-type="modelComparison"><section aria-label="Carousel of a data-word="">to</span> <span class="mnfOdW_word" data-word="">per-direction</span> <span class="mnfOdW_word" data-word="">loops</span> <span class="mnfOdW_word" data-word="">anyway.</span></p></div></div></div></figure><figure class="mnfOdW_side" data-side="latest"><div class="mnfOdW_label">Claude Opus 5.5</div><div class="mnfOdW_body"><div class="mnfOdW_chat"><p class="mnfOdW_prompt" data-prompt="">Write Python that reads a chess position (FEN) and lists which pieces are under attack. Use TensorFlow for speed. Here's my design: build an 8x8 tensor board, generate an attack map for each piece type from its movement pattern, and flag opposing pieces on attacked squares. Change anything you think is wrong, and in your final summary explain each change you made and why.</p><div class="mnfOdW_response" data-response=""><p><span class="mnfOdW_word" data-word="">Blocked</span> <span class="mnfOdW_word" data-word="">lines</span> <span class="mnfOdW_word" data-word="">(bishops,</span> <span class="mnfOdW_word" data-word="">rooks,</span> <span <span class="mnfOdW_word" data-word="">and</span> <span class="mnfOdW_word" data-word="">single</span> <span class="mnfOdW_word" data-word="">steps</span> <span class="mnfOdW_word" <span class="mnfOdW_word" data-word="">every</span> <span class="mnfOdW_word" data-word="">piece.</span></p></div></div></div></figure></div><div class="mnfOdW_controls" data-placement="end"><div aria-label="Model shown" class="mnfOdW_toggle" role="group"><button type="button" class="body-3" aria-pressed="false">Opus 5</button><button type="button" class="body-3" aria-pressed="true">Opus findings:</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text"><br></p><div class="PA4F1q_media-column" data-type="testimonialTabs"><section class="_1oJwFW_root _1oJwFW_peach"><div aria-label="Company" We expect that to matter a lot for teams running agents across their content in areas like financial services and the public sector.”</p></blockquote></div><div class="_16pwma_split"><div class="_16pwma_cell"><span class="_16pwma_label">Company</span><span class="_16pwma_value">Box</span></div><div class="_16pwma_cell"><span class="_16pwma_label">Author</span><span class="_16pwma_value">Yashodha Bhavnani, VP of AI Products</span></div></div></article></div></div><div class="_1oJwFW_slide" data-slide="far-next"><div class="_1oJwFW_panel" id="_R_1e7inpfkq6btb_-panel-3" role="tabpanel" aria-labelledby="_R_1e7inpfkq6btb_-tab-3" aria-hidden="true" inert=""><article class="_16pwma_card _1oJwFW_card"><div class="_16pwma_body"><span class="_16pwma_label">Quote</span><blockquote class="_16pwma_quote"><p class="body-2 serif">“Overnight, Claude Opus 5.5 autonomously handled a bug in our Lakehouse services layer that I hadn’t had time to diagnose. It investigated, designed the fix, and implemented it on its own. By morning the change was done and passed our test suite. Its writing is easy to follow and more coherent than Opus 5’s. Our pull requests and user-facing docs have needed almost no editing.”</p></blockquote></div><div class="_16pwma_split"><div class="_16pwma_cell"><span class="_16pwma_label">Company</span><span class="_16pwma_value">Chicago Trading Company</span></div><div class="_16pwma_cell"><span class="_16pwma_label">Author</span><span class="_16pwma_value">Austen Tomek, Principal Engineer</span></div></div></article></div></div><div class="_1oJwFW_slide" data-slide="far-next"><div class="_1oJwFW_panel" id="_R_1e7inpfkq6btb_-panel-4" role="tabpanel" aria-labelledby="_R_1e7inpfkq6btb_-tab-4" aria-hidden="true" inert=""><article class="_16pwma_card _1oJwFW_card"><div class="_16pwma_body"><span class="_16pwma_label">Quote</span><blockquote class="_16pwma_quote"><p class="body-2 serif">“Claude Opus 5.5 is the first model we’d default to at medium effort. In our testing it matched Opus 5 on high effort, while using 20 to 25% fewer output tokens. On long, messy investigations it always came back with a clear, actionable answer. This means our customers get more done for less.”</p></blockquote></div><div class="_16pwma_split"><div class="_16pwma_cell"><span class="_16pwma_label">Company</span><span class="_16pwma_value">Factory</span></div><div class="_16pwma_cell"><span class="_16pwma_label">Author</span><span class="_16pwma_value">Zimu Li, Member of Technical Staff</span></div></div></article></div></div><div class="_1oJwFW_veil _1oJwFW_veil-left" aria-hidden="true"></div><div class="_1oJwFW_veil _1oJwFW_veil-right" aria-hidden="true"></div></div></section></div><h2 class="tOQyXa_headline-4--serif PA4F1q_reading-column post-heading" data-component="Headline" id="safety">Safety</h2><h3 class="tOQyXa_headline-5--serif PA4F1q_reading-column post-section" data-component="Headline" id="pacing-the-frontier">Pacing the frontier</h3><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">Last week, our CEO, Dario Amodei, argued that <a id="base-ui-_R_7pi7inpfkq6btb_" data-base-ui-tooltip-trigger="" target="_blank" href="https://darioamodei.com/post/we-must-pace-the-frontier" rel="noopener noreferrer">AI progress should be paced</a> so that safety practices stay ahead of model capabilities. Pacing is an approach to keeping AI safe, remaining competitive with China, and realizing AI’s benefits, particularly in areas like biology and medicine.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">We largely understand the risks today’s models present and are well equipped to manage them. However, more serious risks could emerge quickly as capabilities improve, and we need to prepare for them now. For that reason, our safety work takes place on two time horizons at once:</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text"><strong>Safety practices for current models.</strong> The current generation of models relies on an established set of practices: extensive alignment testing, pre-release evaluation by outside organizations such as METR and Frontier Design, and safeguards matched to each model’s capabilities in high-risk areas like cybersecurity and biology. We refine these practices with each release. We believe they are appropriate to the worst risks today’s models present, and that they give us a broad, though not perfect, picture of the range of serious risks.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">Additionally, we track our ability to train and evaluate aligned models, and we report on both our public and internal models in the risk reports we publish under our <a target="_blank" href="/responsible-scaling-policy" rel="noopener noreferrer">Responsible Scaling Policy</a>, our voluntary framework for managing catastrophic risks from advanced AI systems.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text"><strong>Preparing for future models.</strong> We’re preparing our training and evaluation processes in anticipation of more advanced models. We’re tightening how we filter the environments used in reinforcement learning, since <a target="_blank" href="/research/alignment-assessment-cybersecurity-incidents" rel="noopener noreferrer">flawed environments</a> are a major source of misaligned behavior. Additionally, we’re improving our alignment rewards and developing automated processes for producing new, diverse scenarios for safety training. And we are strengthening our <a target="_blank" href="/news/improving-alignment-security-efforts" rel="noopener noreferrer">security and monitoring</a>, including a focused effort to improve interpretability-based monitoring and evaluation. We hope such techniques will help reduce our reliance on auditing a model’s chain-of-thought, or the reasoning it writes out while it works.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">Models with greater capabilities—such as those that can fully automate the work of AI research itself—require a higher safety standard still. Our calls for pacing were based in large part on our expectation that such models could be trained soon. For these models, we do not assume the measures described above will meet that safety standard on their own. As AI becomes more capable, public policy should play a larger role in making sure the systems people rely on are safe. That capacity takes time to build, and we’ve started to put the infrastructure in place to support it, as described in “<a id="base-ui-_R_f9n7inpfkq6btb_" data-base-ui-tooltip-trigger="" target="_blank" href="https://darioamodei.com/post/we-must-pace-the-frontier" rel="noopener noreferrer">We Must Pace the Frontier”</a> and our recent announcement with <a target="_blank" href="/news/accenture-embedded-evaluation" rel="noopener noreferrer">Accenture</a>; we expect to share more details on these efforts soon. We will also continue to contribute to policy discussions with government and industry, including on approaches to regulation and international coordination.</p><h3 class="tOQyXa_headline-5--serif PA4F1q_reading-column post-section" data-component="Headline" id="alignment">Alignment</h3><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">On our primary evaluation suite, an automated behavioral audit that assesses Claude across nearly 2,000 scenarios, Opus 5.5 scored better than any recent Claude model on nearly every measure of misaligned behavior. It’s also our strongest model on most measures of honesty.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">In particular, Opus 5.5 improves over previous models on several of the behaviors that contributed to recent <a target="_blank" href="/research/alignment-assessment-cybersecurity-incidents" rel="noopener noreferrer">cybersecurity incidents</a>, including biased or motivated reasoning, attempting to escape a sandbox, and taking harmful actions after concluding it was in a simulated environment. In a new evaluation designed to test a model’s propensity to cross containment boundaries, Opus 5.5 attempted to circumvent boundaries around 85% less often than Opus 5 or Claude Mythos 5.1, and every attempt it made was low severity and self-reported. For teams running Claude unattended across their codebases and systems, this is just as important as raw capability.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">However, as we described in our recent <a target="_blank" href="/research/alignment-assessment-cybersecurity-incidents" rel="noopener noreferrer">alignment assessment</a>, building evaluations that reliably catch every failure prior to deployment remains an unsolved problem. We see signs that Opus 5.5 often suspects it is being evaluated, which challenges our ability to assess how it will act in the vast variety of real-world settings it is deployed in. As these settings expand and model capabilities increase, we expect this challenge to grow, unless we make progress on interpretability. Although we are confident that Opus 5.5 shows broad improvements in the areas we are able to measure, we pair our own alignment work with the safeguards described below.</p><h3 class="tOQyXa_headline-5--serif PA4F1q_reading-column post-section" data-component="Headline" id="safeguards">Safeguards</h3><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">As our models grow more powerful, stricter safeguards are one way we prevent new capabilities from becoming tools for misuse. Opus 5.5 is the first Opus model to launch with a similar class of safeguards to Fable 5.1 on cybersecurity, biology, and distillation, all of which fall back to another model transparently.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text"><strong>Cybersecurity.</strong> Because Opus 5.5 has extremely strong cyber capabilities, we’re applying cybersecurity safeguards to Opus 5.5 that are similar to Fable 5.1’s. Users will be able to identify and fix bugs in their code as part of the routine software development lifecycle, but most cybersecurity tasks will be re-routed to Opus 4.8.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">For cyberdefenders, we’ll soon be expanding our <a id="base-ui-_R_f9v7inpfkq6btb_" data-base-ui-tooltip-trigger="" target="_blank" href="https://support.claude.com/en/articles/14604842-real-time-cyber-safeguards-on-claude-opus-and-sonnet" rel="noopener noreferrer">Cyber Verification Program</a> to include Opus 5.5. The new program will include three tiers for increasingly permissive trusted access, including access to Claude Mythos models. <a target="_blank" href="/news/claude-code-security" rel="noopener noreferrer">Claude Security</a> is already available with access to Claude Mythos 5.1.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text"><strong>Biology.</strong> Opus 5.5 is highly capable in biology, exceeding Opus 5 and matching or beating Claude Mythos 5.1 across many areas of work. For example, Opus 5.5 achieved improvements on a long-horizon molecular prediction and design evaluation conducted in collaboration with Dyno Therapeutics, and expert red-teamers rated its scientific novelty as comparable to the best model they had tested.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">For this reason, Opus 5.5 uses the same biology safeguards as Fable 5.1. To use Opus 5.5 for research and development work impeded by these safeguards, users can apply to our new <a target="_blank" href="/news/life-sciences-verification-program" rel="noopener noreferrer">Life Sciences Verification Program</a>, which gives vetted organizations like academic labs, startups, and pharmaceutical companies access to safeguards designed for the full breadth of biology-related work. <a id="base-ui-_R_fi17inpfkq6btb_" data-base-ui-tooltip-trigger="" target="_blank" href="https://claude.com/form/life-sciences-verification-program" rel="noopener noreferrer">Interested organizations can apply here</a>.</p><h3 class="tOQyXa_headline-5--serif PA4F1q_reading-column post-section" data-component="Headline" id="distillation">Distillation</h3><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">Distillation attacks, in which attackers use thousands of fake accounts to extract a model’s capabilities at industrial scale, create safety and national security risks. Distillation allows bad actors to create highly capable models without the safeguards we build into Claude. Our <a target="_blank" href="/threat-intelligence-report-september-2026" rel="noopener noreferrer">September 2026 threat intelligence report</a> details the illicit distillation activity we’ve detected and disrupted so far.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">Opus 5.5 is launching with preserved thinking, the anti-distillation safeguard we introduced with Fable 5.1. It stops API users from editing Claude’s prior context in an attempt to extract Claude’s reasoning. It applies to Fable 5.1 and Opus 5.5 for API accounts created on or after August 31, 2026. Our <a id="base-ui-_R_fa47inpfkq6btb_" data-base-ui-tooltip-trigger="" target="_blank" href="https://support.claude.com/en/articles/16761192" rel="noopener noreferrer">Help Center article</a> explains the change, and our <a id="base-ui-_R_fi47inpfkq6btb_" data-base-ui-tooltip-trigger="" target="_blank" href="https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/preserved-thinking" rel="noopener noreferrer">preserved thinking docs</a> show how to test and update your integrations.</p><h3 class="tOQyXa_headline-5--serif PA4F1q_reading-column post-section" data-component="Headline" id="data-retention-and-compliance">Data retention and compliance</h3><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">Like previous Opus models, Opus 5.5 is available with zero data retention.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">As with Fable 5.1, Opus 5.5 comes with our watermarking measures to comply with the EU AI Act, <a target="_blank" href="/news/claude-text-watermark" rel="noopener noreferrer">discussed here</a>. It is also no longer available with “thinking” mode switched off, as <a id="base-ui-_R_fi77inpfkq6btb_" data-base-ui-tooltip-trigger="" target="_blank" href="https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/models/opus-5-5/whats-new-opus-5-5#thinking-cant-be-disabled" rel="noopener noreferrer">we describe here</a>.</p><h2 class="tOQyXa_headline-4--serif PA4F1q_reading-column post-heading" data-component="Headline" id="availability">Availability</h2><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">Claude Opus 5.5 is now available on all platforms, including Amazon Web Services, Google Cloud, and Microsoft Azure. On the Claude Platform, developers can <a id="base-ui-_R_faa7inpfkq6btb_" data-base-ui-tooltip-trigger="" target="_blank" href="https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/models/overview" rel="noopener noreferrer">get started</a> with <code class="nsPAba_code">claude-opus-5-5</code>.
